  📝 Cypher: MATCH (moto:Object)
WHERE (moto.type='motorcycle' OR moto.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle')
WITH moto LIMIT 1
MATCH (wr:Object) WHERE wr.status='with_rider'
MATCH (wr)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(ped:Object)
WHERE ped.type='pedestrian' AND r.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH moto, ped, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
RETURN moto.status = ped.status AS same_status

  📝 Cypher: MATCH (motorcycle:Object)
WHERE (motorcycle.type='motorcycle' OR motorcycle.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle')
MATCH (ego:Object {unique_id:'ego'})
MATCH (motorcycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(target:Object)
MATCH (ego)-[r2:RELATES_TO]->(target)
WHERE r1.predicates[0]='back-right' AND r2.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH motorcycle, ego
MATCH (motorcycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(target)
WHERE r1.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH motorcycle, ego, target, r1
MATCH (ego)-[r2:RELATES_TO]->(target)
WHERE r2.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH target, r1, r2
LIMIT 1
RETURN target.unique_id, target.type, target.status

  📝 Cypher: MATCH (motorcycle:Object)
WHERE (motorcycle.type='motorcycle' OR motorcycle.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle')
- related from the motorcycle by a RELATES_TO relationship with predicates[0]='back-right' (because "back right" is a compound direction)
- and also related from the ego by a RELATES_TO relationship with predicates[0]='front-left' (because "front left" is compound)
MATCH (motorcycle:Object)
WHERE (motorcycle.type='motorcycle' OR motorcycle.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle')
MATCH (ego:Object {unique_id:'ego'})
MATCH (motorcycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(target:Object)
WHERE r1.predicates[0]='back-right' AND target.status='parked'
MATCH (ego)-[r2:RELATES_TO]->(target)
WHERE r2.predicates[0]='front-left'
MATCH (motorcycle:Object)
WHERE (motorcycle.type='motorcycle' OR motorcycle.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle')
MATCH (ego:Object {unique_id:'ego'})
MATCH (motorcycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(target:Object)
WHERE r1.predicates[0]='back-right' AND target.status='parked'
MATCH (ego)-[r2:RELATES_TO]->(target)
WHERE r2.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH target, r1, r2
LIMIT 1
RETURN target.type

WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'

  ⏱️ 耗时: 15.29秒
  💭 AI思维过程:
--------------------------------------------------
    We are given the question: "There is a pedestrian to the back right of the truck; what is its status?"
    
    Steps:
    1. Identify the reference object: "the truck" (which excludes trailers).
    2. Identify the target object: "a pedestrian" (which is to the back right of the truck).
    3. The relationship: "to the back right of" → this is a compound direction (back-right) so we use `r.predicates[0]='back-right'`.
    4. The query type: asking for the status of the pedestrian.
    
    Structure:
    - We need to find a truck (excludes trailers) and then the pedestrian that is in the back-right direction relative to that truck.
    - Since there might be multiple, we order by distance and take the closest one (LIMIT 1) to get "the" pedestrian.
    
    Cypher:
    - Start by matching the truck: `MATCH (truck:Object) WHERE truck.type='t...(省略)
--------------------------------------------------
  📝 Cypher: MATCH (truck:Object) WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
MATCH (truck)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(pedestrian:Object) 
WHERE pedestrian.type='pedestrian' AND r.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH pedestrian, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
RETURN pedestrian.status

    We are given the question: "What is the status of the bicycle to the front left of the truck?"
    
    Steps:
    1. Identify the reference object: "the truck" (which means a truck excluding trailers).
       - We'll find a truck that is not a trailer: `WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'`
       - Since it says "the truck", we assume there is one or we take the first one we find? But note: the bicycle is defined relative to the truck, so we need to find the bicycle that is front-left of the truck.
    
    2. The target object: "the bicycle" that is in the front left of the truck.
       - We are to return the status of that bicycle.

    4. Relationship: 
       - The truck ...(省略)
--------------------------------------------------
  📝 Cypher: MATCH (truck:Object)
WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
MATCH (truck)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(bicycle:Object)
WHERE bicycle.type='bicycle' AND r.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH bicycle, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
RETURN bicycle.status

  问题类型: count
  答案格式: Answer with a number only (e.g., "5").

E:\Project\ADVTEST\.venv310\lib\site-packages\urllib3\connectionpool.py:1097: InsecureRequestWarning: Unverified HTTPS request is being made to host '127.0.0.1'. Adding certificate verification is strongly advised. See: https://urllib3.readthedocs.io/en/latest/advanced-usage.html#tls-warnings
  warnings.warn(
  ⏱️ 耗时: 22.25秒
  💭 AI思维过程:
--------------------------------------------------
    We are given: "How many other pedestrians are in the same status as the pedestrian to the back right of the truck?"
    
    Steps:
    1. Identify the reference objects and the target objects.
       - The question has two parts:
         a) Find a pedestrian that is to the back right of a truck.
         b) Count other pedestrians (excluding that one) that have the same status as that pedestrian.
    
    2. Break down the spatial relationship:
       - "the pedestrian to the back right of the truck": 
            This means the truck is the reference, and the pedestrian is the target.
            So, we match: (truck)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(pedestrian)
            The direction is "back right", which is a compound direction -> use r.predicates[0] = 'back-right'
    
    3. Special type for truck: 
            We need a truck (excluding trailers). So: ...(省略)
--------------------------------------------------
  📝 Cypher: MATCH (truck:Object)
WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
MATCH (truck)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(pedestrian:Object)
WHERE pedestrian.type='pedestrian' AND r.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH pedestrian, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
WITH pedestrian.status AS refStatus, pedestrian.unique_id AS refId
MATCH (other:Object)
WHERE other.type='pedestrian' AND other.status=refStatus AND other.unique_id<>refId
RETURN count(other) AS count

  WHERE moto.type='motorcycle' OR moto.category CONTAINS 'motorcycle'
WITH car, moto LIMIT 1

RETURN moto.status = car.status AS same_status

WITH truck.status AS truckStatus, truck.unique_id AS truckId 
LIMIT 1

WHERE bicycle.type='bicycle' 
  AND bicycle.status = truckStatus 
  AND bicycle.unique_id <> truckId 
RETURN count(bicycle) > 0 AS exist

WHERE car.type = 'car' AND r.predicates[0] = 'back-right'
WITH car, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
RETURN car.status

  📝 Cypher: MATCH (bicycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(truck:Object)
WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
AND r1.predicates[0]='back-right'
MATCH (trailer:Object) WHERE trailer.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
MATCH (bicycle:Object)
WHERE bicycle.type='bicycle' AND bicycle.status='with_rider'
WITH bicycle LIMIT 1
MATCH (bicycle)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(truck:Object)
WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
AND r1.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH truck, r1 ORDER BY r1.distance ASC LIMIT 1
MATCH (trailer:Object)
WHERE trailer.category CONTAINS 'trailer'
WITH trailer, truck LIMIT 1
RETURN trailer.status = truck.status AS same_status

    We are comparing the status of two buses defined by their spatial relationships.
     The first bus: "the bus to the back right of the not standing pedestrian"
       - Reference: a pedestrian that is not standing (pedestrian.status <> 'standing')
       - Direction: 'back-right' (compound direction) → use r.predicates[0] = 'back-right'
       - Target: bus (bus1.type='bus')
    
     The second bus: "the bus that is to the front of the stopped trailer"
       - Reference: a stopped trailer (trailer.category CONTAINS 'trailer' AND trailer.status='stopped')
       - Direction: 'front' (single direction) → use r.direction_4='front'
       - Target: bus (bus2.type='bus')

     Since the question says "the bus" for each, we assume the closest one in the specified direction.

--------------------------------------------------
  📝 Cypher: MATCH (ped:Object) WHERE ped.type='pedestrian' AND ped.status <> 'standing'
MATCH (ped)-[r1:RELATES_TO]->(bus1:Object) WHERE bus1.type='bus' AND r1.predicates[0]='back-right'
WITH ped, bus1, r1 ORDER BY r1.distance ASC LIMIT 1

MATCH (trailer:Object) WHERE trailer.category CONTAINS 'trailer' AND trailer.status='stopped'
MATCH (trailer)-[r2:RELATES_TO]->(bus2:Object) WHERE bus2.type='bus' AND r2.direction_4='front'
WITH bus1, bus2, r2 ORDER BY r2.distance ASC LIMIT 1

RETURN bus1.status = bus2.status AS same_status

            (with_rider_thing)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(truck) 
            WHERE r.predicates[0]='front-left'
    3. We want the status of that truck (call it refStatus) and its id (to exclude it when looking for "other" cars).

  AND r.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH truck, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
WITH truck.status AS refStatus, truck.unique_id AS refId
MATCH (car:Object)
WHERE car.type='car' 
  AND car.status=refStatus 
  AND car.unique_id<>refId

    Question Type: status

    1. Identify the reference object: "the bus"
    2. Identify the target object: "the truck" (which is to the front left of the bus)
    3. We are to return the status of that truck.
    
    Direction: "front left" is a compound direction -> use r.predicates[0] = 'front-left'
    
    Semantics: "truck to the front left of the bus" means:
      - The bus is the reference -> (bus)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(truck)
      - Direction: r.predicates[0] = 'front-left'

            WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer'

      - We don't have any specific constraint on the bus (like status) so we just match any bus.
    
    ...(省略)
--------------------------------------------------
  📝 Cypher: MATCH (bus:Object) WHERE bus.type='bus'
MATCH (bus)-[r:RELATES_TO]->(truck:Object)
WHERE truck.type='truck' AND NOT truck.category CONTAINS 'trailer' AND r.predicates[0]='front-left'
WITH truck, r ORDER BY r.distance ASC LIMIT 1
RETURN truck.status

  📝 Cypher: MATCH (ego:Object {unique_id:'ego'})-[r:RELATES_TO]->(obj:Object)
